TCD-059 leaves open whether it was publicly known before suppression or never meaningfully acknowledged. The sources pull in different directions: The Remnian Timeline Before Story Start describes a "highly publicized and controversial birth" followed by a royal decree making any discussion of it treason, and, in 2091, a Sanctum suppression order; the unaudited Imperial Lineage repeats the birth and the decree. The Character Bible speaks of royal decrees and "whispered scandals." Welcome to Remnia says she was raised with "a name that wasn't hers and a lineage no one dared to say out loud," and that acknowledging her would mean "admitting Tobias violated Imperial marriage law." The audit flags that file's public-scandal framing as unresolved. Nothing in the Beta shows any character treating Katherine as a known scandal. Do not smooth this into "everyone half-knew." It's a real choice with real consequences for how people have looked at Katherine for twenty years. WHO KNOWS WHAT, AT FTE'S OPENING. PERSON OR BODY KNOWS KATHERINE IS TOBIAS'S DAUGHTER? He is an independent cross-land actor with deep Nightmare-adjacent access and nightmare magic, and he is not a member of the Nightmare organization. CANON TCD-031 How it starts (ch. 2). A hooded man in Shadowmere knows a name Melena has told no one. He has silver hair "not dyed," skin like wheat in a Rovencian field, three tiny studs in his ears, each a different metal, and violet eyes. He tells her Elias is in Somnoria as Professor Alexander Thorne, that his cousin Cassius is investigating him, and that the borders are closed "but there are still ways through." He wants her in the Tournament, win or lose, "as long as you keep their eyes on you." The price she gets is direct access to Elias's messages. Atlas's black-vein deterioration begins in the Conduit Trial. CANON TCD-051. He forcesynchronizes his dense, controlled Frost with Katherine's reactive Frost in the same circuit. He tries to impose structure; the Frost overcorrects, turns inward, and creates a progressive injury visible as black veins. It isn't an accumulation of ordinary Element Rot, and no earlier Sanctum corruption, artifact, or experiment caused it. WHAT'S ON THE PAGE. SOURCE STATUS WHAT IT SHOWS Beta ch. 28 CURRENT DRAFTED TEXT Nico briefs the Trial: four pillars, bonded-pair synchronization, runes in white and black. By the chapter's end the black veins reach Atlas's cheekbones. Chapter 29.docx SUPPORTED · rewrite Atlas collapses; his skin is "cold in a way frost shouldn't be — wrong temperature, wrong texture"; black veining creeps toward his eyes and throat. Katherine performs the Elders' Prayer; the veining freezes "mid-crawl," then retreats down his forearms "until it thinned into nothing." FTE 31–39 Character Bible required SUPPORTED · rewrite required SUPPORTED A fading "tether" between Katherine and Atlas, carrying his alarm and thoughts, and Dax's heat; Atlas pale, hollow-eyed, hands bandaged "The most powerful Frost in Old Prosperity"; control "nearabsolute"; the room froze at his birth The Character Bible's Atlas is the reason the ruling works. A Frost defined by control is exactly the Frost who would try to impose structure on someone else's reactive power and get hurt doing it. FTE › IV.16 ATLAS: THE SYNCHRONIZATION INJURY · CONTINUES → IV.16 / 1 · 163 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST IV.16 · ATLAS: THE SYNCHRONIZATION INJURY TSWK CONTINUITY CONTINUED GUARDRAIL No retrofitted history. Atlas's past violence (in ch. 7 Katherine recalls that he nearly killed Minho three years earlier; the Character Bible has a battle that nearly destroyed a mountainside) is not an earlier corruption, and an early description of his veins standing out against his skin is not foreshadowing of a preexisting condition. The injury starts in the Trial. OPEN Does the injury come back? TCD-051 calls it progressive, and the audit asks why it progresses after synchronization ends. Chapter 29.docx has the Prayer drive it out entirely. Both can't be the whole story: either the Prayer stabilizes it without curing it, or it returns, or the progression happens before the Prayer. The Prayer restores a viable magical pattern and can't regenerate destroyed tissue (TCD-057), which leaves room for lasting damage. II.14 The Elders' Prayer · p.42 IV.17 The Elders' Prayer in FTE · p.166 IV.24 FTE Endgame Packet · p.187 FTE › IV.16 ATLAS: THE SYNCHRONIZATION INJURY IV.16 / 3 · 165 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY PERFORMANCE RECORD · PART IV · FOR THEIR ENTERTAINMENT / REMNIA IV.17 THE ELDERS' PRAYER IN FTE Record Katherine's performance. Katherine performs the Elders' Prayer for Atlas. CANON TCD-009. It's the same inherited working Selah performs in 3SL, in a Remnian form, about two years later and with no causal link between the two performances. CANON TCD-023, TCD-048. Its function is fixed: it restores a living person's viable magical pattern when that pattern is severed, corrupted, or catastrophically dysregulated, and it can call someone back from active dying. It can't resurrect completed death or regenerate destroyed anatomy. CANON TCD-057 (II.14) The controlling generation hasn't drafted it. Chapter 29.docx has, as a candidate ( SUPPORTED · rewrite required), and it gets a great deal right. The candidate scene. Atlas collapses in the Conduit Trial. Katherine laces her fingers through his and presses their palms flat, "forcing contact the way her mother had taught her. Press here. Hold here. Eyes closed. Don't let go." Her Frost pours into him and doesn't behave like her power: "It didn't bite outward. It went into him." His Frost "reached back. Barely." Lines write themselves up both their forearms, matching, permanent. The black veining stops, then retreats. The Prayer takes long enough that London Town claims its second conduit, and Old Prosperity loses the Challenge. The words, as the candidate draft has them. This is manuscript text in a rewrite-required file, not canon wording: By Elder's right. By covenant sworn— / I stake my claim. / Frost to frost, I bind your name— / For life we'll share. Through better or worse— / Let death know my name first. CHECKED AGAINST THE RULINGS. The Prayer happens on live broadcast. The announcers call it "an ancient Frost binding ritual. Once begun, it cannot be stopped," and "something we have never seen before in a Tournament"; the crowd chants KATHERINE! ATLAS! That's exactly the kind of institutional misreading II.15 tracks: Remnia's public knows it as a Frost bond, and its objective function is broader. If this staging survives, the Prayer stops being a family secret the moment Katherine performs it, and every viewer who knows the old rite now knows she knows it. Minho's death is not assumed. CANON TCD-009. The legacy draft killed him, and the ruling retired that: his current arc has to be found first, and death may only return if it becomes earned and indispensable to his own arc or to Tandem's consequences. That makes Minho the least-settled major character in FTE and the one most at risk of being written by momentum. WHAT THE SOURCES ESTABLISH. Old Prosperity wins because London Town forfeits after Challenge Six. CANON TCD-009. The forfeit is an act of agency on three levels at once, and the rulings keep the levels apart. CANON TCD-042, TCD-052 The causal chain. Every link is ruled; none is drafted. # LINK WHO RULING 1 Katherine's Frost configuration catastrophically fails; the older capacity reroutes as Fire Katherine TCD-058 2 Katherine's final sanctioned action against or for Melena prevents Katherine TCD-042 3 That action creates the decisive state Katherine TCD-034, TCD-042 4 Melena consciously chooses survival over victory, rather than Melena TCD-042, TCD-052 Tiran TCD-042, TCD-052 6 Melena brings the decision to Maxim, or endorses it to him Melena → Maxim TCD-052 7 Maxim, alive, conscious, and acting as King, formally enters or Maxim TCD-052 The Sanctum TCD-042, TCD-052 — TCD-034 catastrophe and saves or stabilizes Melena sacrificing more people to reach Challenge Seven 5 Tiran advises, supports, and operationalizes her choice; he can't surrender the province on his own authority authorizes the forfeit 8 The Sanctum accepts and formalizes it; it may exploit the outcome but doesn't compel it 9 Katherine is Conqueror Atlas and Tiran's confrontation happens in the same climax and is officially a draw by intervention. CANON TCD-034. The sister reveal's confrontation stage lands here too (IV.13). Tournament Command Authority. What each rank can do, as far as the rulings go. FTE › IV.20 THE ENDGAME: FORFEIT AND COMMAND AUTHORITY · CONTINUES → IV.20 / 1 · 176 SPINE 3SL ADST FTE TSWK IV.20 · THE ENDGAME: FORFEIT AND COMMAND AUTHORITY CONTINUITY CONTINUED RANK CAN CAN'T OPEN King Formally enter or authorize a provincial forfeit (Maxim) — Whether the King alone holds that power in every case Queen Make the substantive decision and bring it to the King (Melena) Surrender the province on her own, as far as ruled Her emergency authority if the King is down Bishop Advise, support, operationalize (Tiran) Surrender the province on rank alone Anything beyond that Any Piece Surrender individually in a bout (31–39: a verbal yield) — The difference between individual yield and provincial forfeit The Sanctum Accept, verify, formalize Be the original cause of this forfeit Its veto NOT THE SAME AS Tournament command and military command. In 31–39 ch. 39, Minho names Corporal Katherine his successor for the En Guard and Atlas names her for the Legion, citing a protocol that lets a commanding officer name a successor regardless of rank. That's Old Prosperity's military chain, not Tournament authority. It doesn't give Katherine the power to forfeit or accept a forfeit, and it doesn't change who formally acts for London Town. SUPERSEDED The legacy ending centered Tiran's announcement and incapacitated Maxim to make room for it. Retired by TCD-042 and TCD-052. Melena as the breaking villain who hands Katherine the win is retired by TCD-009. Why Challenge Seven matters here. Melena's choice is a choice against something. The reader has to know what continuing costs: which Pieces would have to die, what the Sanctum has promised or threatened, what the Seventh would have been. Without that, the forfeit reads as collapse instead of agency (IV.7). A / 1 · 223 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK APPENDIX A · CANON DECISION LOG · FTE SLICE CONTINUITY CONTINUED ID · DATE TOPIC · BOOKS AUTHOR RULING SUPERSEDES · DOWNSTREAM TCD-004 Graveshand/Deat hwork classification Graveshand is inherited Graves-family magic outside the formal Six Disciplines. Deathwork is the broader category of ending-magic/effects that Graveshand can perceive, manipulate, sever, redirect, or create. SUPERSEDES Any treatment of The complete Shattered Veil is canon at the author/universe level. The four cultural texts are canon divergent inherited versions. Characters do not automatically possess or know the complete master text; intact in-world custody was initially left open and is resolved by TCD-047. SUPERSEDES Any assumption The Tournament is structured around seven Challenges, but reaching Challenge Seven is not guaranteed. Attrition, Checkmate, disqualification, intervention, or another decisive outcome may end it early. In current FTE, the Sanctum declares Challenge Six decisive and terminates the Tournament. SUPERSEDES Any statement that The Conduit Trials replaces Burden and Bonds as the current Challenge Four concept. A / 15 · 237 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK APPENDIX A · CANON DECISION LOG · FTE SLICE CONTINUITY CONTINUED ID · DATE TOPIC · BOOKS AUTHOR RULING SUPERSEDES · DOWNSTREAM TCD-045 Historical nature of the Divide The Divide is a two-stage historical crisis. First, a non-deliberate natural, celestial, cosmic, or magical rupture destabilized the primordial system and relations among the lands. Ancient peoples then created, adapted, bound, activated, or repurposed seals, artifacts, routes, living systems, and boundary structures in response. Their deliberate response did not cause the rupture, but it hardened its consequences into longterm separation. “Divide” may name the initiating catastrophe or the broader rupture–containment–hardening era. Long isolation accelerated specialization of one shared magical ancestry; migration may be a consequence, not the cause. SUPERSEDES Divide as solely Convergence is the progressive destabilization and re-entanglement of the four lands, magical branches, boundary systems, buried shared history, routes, artifacts, and entities after enforced separation. It can manifest through permeability, resonance, cross-land responses, restored contact, knowledge leakage, and failing isolation structures. It is a transition state, neither inherently repair nor destruction; present choices determine whether the result is controlled reconnection, transformed partial separation, restabilization, catastrophic collapse, or another state. It does not physically merge continents or flatten distinct magics. The Shattered Veil predicts compatible conditions, but its reliability is not yet fixed. This is Build 2 of the Tandem Companion: a standalone working volume for For Their Entertainment and Remnia. It carries Part I (how the Companion works) in full, the Part II sections FTE depends on, and all of Part IV, with working pages for IV.10 and IV.25 and a rebuilt working record of the Remnian imperial and royal lineage after IV.11. The copy is the locked Stage 3 Content Master; this volume lays it out and adds working pages. It doesn’t add canon. Some pages point to sections that live only in the full Tandem Companion. Two conventions keep those pointers honest: Continuity Room and 3SL pointers resolve here. References to VII.1, VII.2, VII.5, VII.7, VII.8, VII.10, VII.12 and Part VII, and to the 3SL sections III.3, III.13–III.16, III.18, III.20 and Part III, lead to the FTE Continuity Room concordance near the end of this volume, which names where the FTE information already sits in these pages. Everything else outside the volume carries a dagger (†). A reference marked † is to a section of the full Tandem Companion that isn’t printed here. Page numbers. “See also” chips give the page where each in-volume section starts. CORRECTIONS APPLIED IN THIS VOLUME Two author status corrections post-date the Content Master and are applied where the claims appear (IV.2, IV.3). The lineage reconstruction also corrected one status and several source attributions in IV.11, IV.12 and IV.22; each is logged in the QA report with the exact text it replaces. Rulings without a TCD number are listed in Appendix A. BUILD 2 › ABOUT THIS VOLUME · CONTINUES → ABOUT / 1 · 2 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK ABOUT THIS VOLUME CONTINUITY CONTINUED AUTHOR CORRECTION · 2026-10-09 · IV.2 AND IV.3 CANON Remnia is an empire on its own continental landmass, separated from Somnoria by ocean; the Crown sits at the highest public civil/dynastic level of the Remnian sovereignty structure. TCD-025 · TCD-041 Remnia has seventeen provinces. FTE Beta (ch. 2 and two further passages; IV.2) The military structure (the Legion, the Vanguard, the En Guard). TCD-041 is the layered sovereignty framework, not the military’s place in it. FTE Beta chs. 1, 5, 26 DRAFTED DRAFTED THE LINEAGE RECORD The pages after IV.11 rebuild the Remnian imperial and royal lineage from the sources, claim by claim. Each claim carries its source and the status that source earns. Biology, legal family status, the public record, titles and succession are kept in separate layers. The record is a derived working record, not a new source, and it settles nothing the rulings leave open. The unaudited Imperial Lineage was used only for comparison. WHAT ISN’T IN THIS VOLUME Part III and Parts V–VII (3SL, ADST, TSWK and the full Continuity Room); the Part II sections FTE doesn’t stand on (II.1, II.3, II.7–II.12); and Appendix D. The appendices printed here are FTE slices: the rulings, conflicts, sources and terms this volume cites. under ten minutes. Every later Part assumes you know what a status marker permits, which source wins in which domain, and why a character can't know something just because you do. § SECTION PAGE TYPE I.1 What the Tandem Companion Is ORIENTATION 7 I.2 The Manuscript Outranks the Companion RULE 10 I.3 How Source Authority Works SOURCE AUTHORITY 12 I.4 Reading a Claim: Status Markers STATUS KEY 15 I.5 Knowledge States EXPLAINER 18 I.6 Open Canon, Decisions, and Updates PROTOCOL 21 I.7 Using the Companion While Drafting CHECKLIST 24 SPINE › PART I PAGE PART I · 6 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY ORIENTATION · PART I · HOW THIS COMPANION WORKS I.1 WHAT THE TANDEM COMPANION IS Frame the book as a working writer-room reference, not a wiki or workbook. The Tandem Companion is the working reference for four novels set in one physical world: 3 Shadowmere Lane, For Their Entertainment, A Dream So Twisted, and The Sea Will Keep. It holds the canon those books have already committed to, the rulings that settled their contradictions, and the open questions they haven't answered yet. It sits beside the manuscript while you draft. It isn't a replacement for it. The Companion does five jobs. It remembers what's been ruled and drafted so you don't have to reread three generations of files to find out. It distinguishes things a drafter will blur: the Nightmare organization from the ancient entity, Katherine's Frost from Zephyr's, the forensic record of Remy's death from Melena's guilt about it. It connects the books only where a ruling or source connects them. It protects chronology and knowledge, so nobody in 3SL behaves as if they've read ADST. And it catches resurrected ideas, the retired concepts that keep sneaking back because they were once written down with confidence. It refuses a few jobs on purpose. It won't fill a gap because the gap looks awkward in print. It won't pick the nicer version of a conflict. It won't invent a sigil, a motto, a date, a map coordinate, or a line of scripture to make a page feel finished. It isn't a fan wiki, a marketing synopsis, or a workbook of blank lines waiting for you to do the thinking. When the sources don't know something, the Companion says so plainly, and that sentence is worth more than a confident guess. TSWK has no draft at all, so its most detailed plan is still only a plan. The same kind of question gets a different kind of answer depending on the row. SOURCE SEE ALSO Audit, Source Inventory; TCD-001, TCD-002, TCD-003, TCD-012; 3SL draft chs. 1–12 (POV headings); FTE Beta chs. 8, 18 (POV headings); ADST ch. 18 (POV heading). I.2 The Manuscript Outranks the Companion · p.10 I.3 How Source Authority Works · p.12 SPINE › I.1 WHAT THE TANDEM COMPANION IS I.1 / 3 · 9 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY RULE · PART I · HOW THIS COMPANION WORKS I.2 THE MANUSCRIPT OUTRANKS THE COMPANION State the single rule that keeps the Companion subordinate to the books. Drafted prose controls drafted events. If a chapter in the controlling generation shows something happening, it happened, and the Companion records it. If that chapter deliberately changes something the Companion already printed, the Companion changes. A printed page never gets to veto a scene. That rule has limits that matter as much as the rule. The manuscript controls what it actually shows: POV, scene order, revealed identities, performed magic, relationships, and immediate consequences. It doesn't automatically control national geography, deep history, or magical taxonomy just because a character says something about them. When Silas thinks nobody born in the Underside carries fire, that's a drafted fact about what Silas believes. It becomes worldlevel canon only if a ruling or the governing worldbuilding agrees. A manuscript can also be wrong by accident. A typo, a stray name from an older generation, or a detail that contradicts a ruling isn't a deliberate change; it's a drafting flag, and the Companion logs it as one instead of rewriting canon around it. Open and provisional material works the other way. Where the Companion marks something OPEN or SUPPORTED / PROVISIONAL, drafting is allowed to settle it. When a scene commits to an answer on purpose, that commitment becomes the new record, and the Companion's update protocol (I.6) moves the old version to the Legacy Recovery Index rather than deleting it. GUARDRAIL Never bend a scene to match a Companion page. If the scene is right and the page is wrong, fix the page. If the scene contradicts an author ruling (a TCD), stop and decide which one you mean; don't let either quietly win. Why it matters: the Companion is only trustworthy if it can't become a second, rival manuscript. The moment a printed entry starts dictating prose, the books stop being written and start being transcribed from an index. SPINE › I.2 THE MANUSCRIPT OUTRANKS THE COMPANION · CONTINUES → I.2 / 1 · 10 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST I.2 · THE MANUSCRIPT OUTRANKS THE COMPANION SOURCE SEE ALSO TSWK CONTINUITY CONTINUED Audit, Preliminary Domain-Based Source Authority Map, rule 2; Production Master A1; 3SL draft ch. 3 (Silas's belief about Underside fire). I.6 Open Canon, Decisions, and Updates · p.21 VII.12 Open Canon Queue → FTE concordance · p.216 SPINE › I.2 THE MANUSCRIPT OUTRANKS THE COMPANION I.2 / 2 · 11 3SL SPINE FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY SOURCE AUTHORITY · PART I · HOW THIS COMPANION WORKS I.3 HOW SOURCE AUTHORITY WORKS Teach the domain-specific ladder so a reader knows why a claim is trusted. Authority is domain-specific. A source can settle one kind of fact and have no say over another. The 3SL draft is the last word on what happens in Dante Mercer's office in chapter 3; it isn't the last word on how far Crownlight sits above sea level. The Thalara worldbuilding file governs Thalaran geography; it can't overrule a ruling about the Drowning. Every claim in this book is trusted because of the rung it stands on in its own domain. THE AUTHORITY LADDER RUNG SOURCE TYPE WHAT IT CONTROLS WHAT IT CAN'T DO 1 Explicit author rulings (Canon Decision Log, TCD-001 onward) Every domain a ruling addresses Nothing outranks it except a later ruling 2 Current manuscript pages What happened on-page: POV, scene chronology, revealed identity, performed magic, relationships, immediate consequences Settle geography, history, or taxonomy by a character's sayso 3 Locked constraints (Novel Constraints, as corrected by rulings) Intended premise and undrafted core engine Override a drafted scene or a later ruling 4 Current outlines and chapter plans Undrafted plot, once their generation is confirmed Overwrite anything already drafted 5 Current book-specific worldbuilding, character, magic, and institutional files Geography, institutions, culture, baseline magic, backstory where the manuscript is silent Contradict a ruling or an on-page event 6 Shared Tandem lore Its claims one at a time, only where they survive review Serve as a blanket master plan 7 Supporting artifacts (timelines, placement tables, genealogy workbooks, poems, quote maps) Their narrow domain, after consistency checks Fill gaps outside that domain 8 Marketing files Positioning language Establish any canon SPINE › I.3 HOW SOURCE AUTHORITY WORKS · CONTINUES → I.3 / 1 · 12 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY I.3 · HOW SOURCE AUTHORITY WORKS CONTINUED RUNG SOURCE TYPE WHAT IT CONTROLS WHAT IT CAN'T DO 9 Legacy drafts and plans Creative history and recovery leads Answer a current question 10 TKC Companion materials Structural and philosophical models only Contribute any Tandem canon, ever Two rules sit above the ladder. First, the Tandem Source Authority Audit's rulings override conflicting source material wherever they speak. Second, filenames and dates confer nothing. "FINAL," "Official," "New," and a recent year are labels a file picked up along the way. FTE 2025 FINAL is Legacy. New FTE Chapters predates the modern Remnia execution. A later date on the TSWK blueprint doesn't make it a ruling. Worked example: the Beta versus the 2025 FINAL. FTE 2025 FINAL is a complete 47chapter manuscript. The FTE Official Beta Reader Copy stops at chapter 29. A reader who trusts completeness picks the FINAL and gets the ending for free. TCD-001 says no: the Beta is the controlling prose generation, and nothing outside it becomes canon until it's rewritten into that generation. The FINAL still earns its shelf space as planning evidence for the unfinished half, but its simple Dyad/Crossblood explanation, its reveal shortcut, its cause for Atlas's deterioration, and its Tiran-only forfeit are all superseded by TCD-049 through TCD-052. The complete file lost to the incomplete one because the incomplete one is the book being written. The Companion treats each as the audited generation because its content matches the audit's description. The Imperial Lineage appears in the Project but not in the audit inventory, so it holds no assigned authority until it's classified; nothing in Parts I–III relies on it. Why it matters: most canon errors in a four-book universe aren't invented facts. They're real facts pulled from the wrong generation or the wrong domain. The ladder is how the Companion answers "which file?" before it answers "what's true?" Appendix C Source Authority Inventory · p.260 SPINE › I.3 HOW SOURCE AUTHORITY WORKS I.3 / 3 · 14 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY STATUS KEY · PART I · HOW THIS COMPANION WORKS I.4 READING A CLAIM: STATUS MARKERS Teach the eight markers and the wording each allows. A status marker attaches to a claim, not to a page. One dossier page can hold settled canon, a drafted fact, a plan, and an open question side by side, and each keeps its own label. The marker also controls the grammar. A POSSIBLE claim written in confident present tense is a canon error even with the POSSIBLE sitting next to it, because readers believe sentences before they believe badges. STATUS KEY MARKER MEANING PERMITTED WORDING FORBIDDEN WORDING CANON Established by an author ruling or a controlling source in its domain Plain declarative present tense, with the TCD or source cited Hedging that makes settled canon look optional CURRENT DRAFTED TEXT Established on-page in the controlling manuscript generation "On-page, …" · "As drafted, …" · "In ch. 7, …" Treating it as immune to revision SUPPORTED / PROVISIONAL A current plan, bible, blueprint, or artifact that hasn't become drafted canon "The outline plans…" · "The blueprint proposes…" · "The worldbuilding file describes…" Present-tense narration of events as though they happened POSSIBLE A live candidate or recovery idea, not approved "A candidate is…" · "One viable reading…" · "If recovered, …" "Is," "will," or "does" about the story world OPEN Unresolved; an author decision is needed if and when drafting forces it State what's fixed, then name exactly what isn't Filling the gap with a plausible answer CONFLICT Current sources disagree and no ruling settles it Name both sources and both claims Picking the nicer version silently SPINE › I.4 READING A CLAIM: STATUS MARKERS · CONTINUES → I.4 / 1 · 15 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK I.4 · READING A CLAIM: STATUS MARKERS MARKER RESEARCH LEGACY / SUPERSEDED CONTINUITY CONTINUED MEANING PERMITTED WORDING FORBIDDEN WORDING Needs outside factual research State the research question Presenting a guess as fact Creative history that no longer controls Past tense, source named: "The 2025 FINAL had…" Using it to answer a current question HOW TO READ A CLAIM: SIX WORKED EXAMPLES CLAIM AS PRINTED STATUS WHY IT'S LABELED THIS WAY Katherine Nicholas is the current Tournament's Conqueror. CANON · TCD-034 An author ruling fixes it, so it takes plain present tense even though the scene that earns it isn't drafted yet. The ruling controls the outcome; the manuscript still has to build the causal path. On-page, Katherine's Examination puts her through severe Fire trauma and still produces only Frost. CURRENT DRAFTED TEXT · FTE The Beta shows it. TCD-058 later explains why it happens (dominant Frost configuration, no ordinary stress trigger), but the event itself is anchored in drafted prose. The July 2026 blueprint plans Azrine's ordination near the end of Book One. Beta; TCD-058 SUPPORTED / PROVISIONAL · blueprint, per audit HC-061 The most detailed TSWK plan proposes it. No ruling has adopted it, so it's written as a plan, never as "Azrine is ordained." The Conduit Trials' final canon name hasn't been chosen. OPEN · TCD-008 The concept and mechanics are current. The name is a working title, and every page that uses it flags it. The blueprint's chapter 29 credits "Cael Dren" with designing the sacrificial route and First Anchor, while its chapters 38–39 identify twelve-year-old Caelen as the first sacrificed Anchor. CONFLICT · HC-059 Two passages in one plan disagree. The Companion names both and protects TCD-064's twelve-year-old Caelen without deciding who designed the route. LEGACY / SUPERSEDED · TCD-027 Past tense, source named. Its seal, spiral or mirrored glyph, and ancient-crossing imagery may come back only as POSSIBLE. RESEARCH appears when a scene needs real-world fact the sources can't supply. The clearest current case is the Remy death scene: once the weapon and wound are chosen, the blood-loss timing Melena works against is a research question, not a canon question (TCD-063 leaves weapon and anatomy open). PAGE FURNITURE YOU'LL SEE THROUGHOUT Source — lines close each section. They're margin provenance in the manuscript and become small-type margin notes in layout. GUARDRAIL boxes hold locked constraints a drafter could break without noticing. NOT THE SAME AS callouts separate paired terms readers merge. OPEN DEVELOPMENT boxes carry four lines: Fixed / Undecided / Why it matters / Decide by. TANDEM CANON DECISION blocks appear only where a page genuinely can't proceed. See also lines point to related sections by number. Why it matters: the status system is the reason this book can be trusted at 2 a.m. in the middle of a chapter. If a claim has no marker and no qualifying grammar, you're meant to be able to rely on it without checking. Appendix A Canon Decision Log · p.223 SPINE › I.4 READING A CLAIM: STATUS MARKERS Appendix E Terminology Register · p.267 I.4 / 3 · 17 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY EXPLAINER · PART I · HOW THIS COMPANION WORKS KNOWLEDGE STATES I.5 Prevent backward knowledge promotion. No later revelation travels backward into an earlier head. That sounds obvious until the fourth book is drafted and a character in the first one starts making choices that only make sense if she's read the Canon Decision Log. The Companion prevents that by keeping seven lanes of knowledge apart wherever a secret, identity, reveal, or piece of history is involved. THE SEVEN LANES LANE WHAT IT RECORDS EXAMPLE OF THE LANE IN USE Objective truth What is true in the universe The gang leader's wound kills Remy (TCD063). Author knowledge What Danyelle has ruled or knows, whether or not anyone on-page does Katherine and Melena are paternal halfsisters through Tobias (TCD-040), long before either sister proves it. Reader knowledge What a reader knows at a specific point in a specific book Tracked per chapter in each book's knowledge ledger; never assumed from author knowledge. Character knowledge What a named character knows, and from when Melena enters the Challenge Six Duel with substantial suspicion about Katherine and no proof (TCD-050). Character belief What a character thinks is true when it's wrong or incomplete Early Reverie believes she's an illegal, unlicensed Dreamwalker with a rare gift; she's also a natural conduit (TCD-029). Institutional / public version What Houses, the Sanctum, the Synod, Skyhold, schools, or governments teach or record The Sanctum's likely "Dyad" label for Katherine, which is incomplete (TCD-049). Suppressed / rewritten What's sealed, censored, misfiled, mythologized, redacted, or lost House Dren's willing-adult First Anchor mythology versus twelve-year-old Caelen (TCD-064). The six levels of cross-land knowledge. Knowing another land exists doesn't mean knowing where it is, how its magic works, how to reach it, what its history is, or how the four lands actually relate. TCD-016 separates these into six levels, and none implies another: SPINE › I.5 KNOWLEDGE STATES · CONTINUES → I.5 / 1 · 18 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY I.5 · KNOWLEDGE STATES CONTINUED 1. Existence — the land is real. 2. Location — where it is. 3. Functional — how its people, magic, or institutions work. 4. Route — how to get there. 5. Historical — what happened between the lands. 6. True Tandem knowledge — the objective relationship among all four. A drafted 3SL example shows why the ladder matters. In chapter 11, Remy jokes that the only place Melena can't reach the blackroot is Somnoria, and Silas hopes she won't ask what Somnoria is. On-page, that establishes that both men know Somnoria by name. It doesn't establish that either knows where it is, how to get there, or anything about dream magic. In chapter 3, Silas reads Melena's fire as proof she's Remnian because "fire belonged to Remnia." That's functional knowledge about one feature of Remnian magic, held as Silas's belief, and it says nothing about what the Synod teaches or what Silas knows of Remnian history. CURRENT DRAFTED TEXT (3SL chs. 3, 11). Worked example: two clocks for one missing man. Elias Lockwood and Professor Alexander Thorne are one person with two public lives, and the two lives go missing on different schedules. LANE ELIAS LOCKWOOD / PROFESSOR ALEXANDER THORNE STATUS Objective truth Elias voluntarily created and maintained Thorne as an established Somnorian researcher and instructor for years. The Nightmare organization targets Thorne's boundary/Network research and exploits the vulnerability it creates, compromising his dream and magical functioning so that ordinary return to Remnia becomes unsafe or impossible. CANON TCD032, TCD-062 Author knowledge The failure-to-return cause is fixed. The exact compromise technique, first crossing, alias credentials, helpers, later captivity, and physical status are not. Institutional / public version, Remnia Remnia notices Elias missing first, because he fails to return on pattern. They reflect a real, patterned compromise, not the whole truth. 062 Character knowledge Reverie encounters Thorne's collapsing dream. CANON TCD- CANON TCD- Encounter CANON TCD062; identity knowledge OPEN Reader knowledge Set chapter by chapter in the ADST and FTE knowledge ledgers (V.16†, IV.22). Tracked, not assumed Why it matters: the two clocks are the whole trick of this plotline. Remnia is already searching while Somnoria still sees Thorne teaching. Collapse the lanes and one country reacts to news it hasn't received. GUARDRAIL In-world language survives as perspective, never as cosmology. A Somnorian calling Remnia "another world" is Somnorian framing (TCD-015). House Aether calling the Prism a "cage" is House Aether's interpretation (TCD-038). Caelum calling Melena his "asset" is Caelum's framing (TCD-043). II.4 Knowledge of the Other Lands · p.29 VII.5 Cross-Land Knowledge Matrix → FTE concordance · p.216 VII.10 Continuity Translation Pages → FTE concordance · p.216 SPINE › I.5 KNOWLEDGE STATES I.5 / 3 · 20 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY PROTOCOL · PART I · HOW THIS COMPANION WORKS I.6 OPEN CANON, DECISIONS, AND UPDATES Show how open material is displayed and how rulings enter the book. Open stays open. The Companion doesn't close a question because a page looks unfinished with it, and it doesn't ask for a ruling just because the book reached that topic. It asks only when a page, a scene, or a publication-level commitment can't be written accurately without an answer. The OPEN DEVELOPMENT box. Wherever an open point sits beside settled canon, it gets a four-line box. Here's a live one, reproduced from Part II: OPEN DEVELOPMENT · THE REMNIA–UNDERSIDE CROSSING · OPEN FIXED UNDECIDED A current crossing exists near the London Town edge, in the no-man's-land associated with London Town, Old Prosperity, and Alea. It's physical-magical transit, not a portal. The singular Echo Gate is Legacy. (TCD-017, TCD027) Its precise map coordinate, and whether it arrives directly in Shadowmere, Tier 7. WHY IT MATTERS DECIDE BY Arrival tier decides who controls the Underside end, who can trace a crossing, and how Melena's route from Remnia into Shadowmere reads on-page. When Danyelle's Remnia map is reviewed, or earlier if a 3SL scene has to show the arrival point. The "Decide by" line is the useful one. It names the actual drafting moment that forces the answer. If nothing forces it, the line says "safe to defer," and that's a complete answer. The TANDEM CANON DECISION block. When a page truly can't proceed, the Companion uses this format and nothing looser: the exact issue, the sources creating it, options A through D where the evidence offers them, E for "Something else / I want to explain," one line of implication under each option, and a labeled recommendation only when the evidence strongly favors one path. No more than five at once; one to three is better. A recommendation is never canon until ruled. SPINE › I.6 OPEN CANON, DECISIONS, AND UPDATES · CONTINUES → I.6 / 1 · 21 3SL SPINE FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY I.6 · OPEN CANON, DECISIONS, AND UPDATES CONTINUED TANDEM CANON DECISION — SPECIMEN (FORMAT ONLY; NOT ASKED NOW) Issue: Who designed First Shellward's sacrificial route and First Anchor? Sources: July 2026 TSWK blueprint ch. 29 ("Cael Dren" as designer) versus chs. 38–39 (twelveyear-old Caelen as first sacrificed Anchor); TCD-064. A. Forged credit. House record credits a figure who didn't design it. Implication: the tablet becomes evidence of rewritten history. B. Distinct adult designer. Cael Dren and Caelen are different people. Implication: two names need separate provenance. C. Drafting error. The blueprint conflated names. Implication: one passage is corrected; no inworld lie. E. Something else / I want to explain. Recommendation: None. The evidence doesn't strongly favor one option, and HC-059 is deferred to the TSWK draft. How a ruling enters the book. When a new ruling lands or drafted prose deliberately changes something: 1. Log it in the Canon Decision Log with its date, what it supersedes, and its downstream implications. 2. Update the High-Impact Conflict Register if it resolves or opens a conflict. 3. Update every Companion page listed in the ruling's affected-sections field. 4. Move superseded claims to the Legacy Recovery Index rather than deleting them. 5. Re-run the integrity checks on every touched page: no Legacy claim presented as canon, no POSSIBLE or SUPPORTED claim in declarative story-world tense, no conflict silently harmonized, no knowledge promoted backward, no symmetry assumed across the four lands, no duplicate or contradictory table, no invented canon, no open question phrased as settled, no TKC content imported. GUARDRAIL Asking a question assigns no TCD number. The audit's proposed TCD-065 through TCD-074 were never answered or adopted, so none of them is canon and none of those numbers is reserved. SPINE › I.6 OPEN CANON, DECISIONS, AND UPDATES · CONTINUES → I.6 / 2 · 22 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST I.6 · OPEN CANON, DECISIONS, AND UPDATES TSWK CONTINUITY CONTINUED Why it matters: an open question that's clearly marked is safe to draft around. An open question that's been quietly answered by whoever wrote the page last is how three versions of the same backstory end up in print. VII.12 Open Canon Queue → FTE concordance · p.216 Appendix A Canon Decision Log · p.223 Appendix B High-Impact Conflict Register · p.251 SPINE › I.6 OPEN CANON, DECISIONS, AND UPDATES I.6 / 3 · 23 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY CHECKLIST · PART I · HOW THIS COMPANION WORKS I.7 USING THE COMPANION WHILE DRAFTING Make the book fast to use mid-scene. Start with the book's draft-forward packet, not the front of the Companion. Each dossier ends with pages built to sit open beside the manuscript: III.19† for 3SL, IV.24 for FTE, V.17† for ADST, VI.13† for TSWK. They condense the locked beats, the current knowledge states, and the scene-level open choices, and they cross-reference the full sections behind them. BEFORE A SCENE Check the packet for any GUARDRAIL that touches the characters or magic in the scene. Check the knowledge ledger for what each POV character knows at this chapter, not at the end of the book. Check whether the scene touches a CANON ruling. If it does, the outcome is fixed and the scene's job is to earn it. Check whether the scene touches an keep drafting. OPEN item. If the open item doesn't affect this scene, WHILE DRAFTING An open question that doesn't touch the next scene doesn't stop the next scene. If the scene needs an answer to an open item, make the choice in prose and flag it. You don't need a ruling first; you need a record afterward. If the scene seems to need something that contradicts a ruling, stop. That's the one case where the draft waits. Don't pull a fact from a Legacy file to fill a gap, however well it fits. Check the Legacy Recovery Index; if the idea is listed there as recoverable, it comes back as POSSIBLE and gets ruled on like anything new. SPINE › I.7 USING THE COMPANION WHILE DRAFTING · CONTINUES → I.7 / 1 · 24 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY I.7 · USING THE COMPANION WHILE DRAFTING CONTINUED AFTER THE SCENE If the prose settled an open item on purpose, log it through the update protocol (I.6). If the prose changed a knowledge state, update the book's knowledge ledger. If the prose introduced a new name, place, route, or rule, add it to the relevant dossier with its chapter as provenance. Navigation conventions. Sections are numbered Part.section (III.8†, VII.5), so insertions don't renumber the book. "See also" lines at each section's foot point outward. Rulings are cited by TCD number, conflicts by HC number, and manuscript facts by book and chapter. Why it matters: the Companion should cost you seconds mid-scene, not an evening. If using it slows drafting down more than it protects the draft, the page is built wrong, and that's a Companion problem to fix. SOURCE SEE ALSO Production Master I.7, A8; Audit, Architecture Handoff. IV.1 Book Identity and Source Status · p.51 IV.24 FTE Endgame Packet · p.187 IV.25 FTE Open Development · p.190 FTE › IV.21 PLOT STATE IV.21 / 2 · 180 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY KNOWLEDGE LEDGER · PART IV · FOR THEIR ENTERTAINMENT / REMNIA IV.22 KNOWLEDGE PROGRESSION Who knows what, when, across the book. FTE is two women learning, from opposite directions, that the official story about their family is wrong. It's also an Empire learning, in public, that one of its Queens has an element she shouldn't. Those are different knowledge problems, and the ledger keeps them separate. Seed ledger. Dated to where it's shown; nothing is promoted to a whole society. Katherine is a soldier who has been told her whole life that she's exactly what she looks like: Michelle Nicholas's daughter, a Frost, a Corporal with a rank she has to keep earning. Every one of those things is true. None of them is complete. The book is the slow discovery that the fence around her life was built on purpose (ch. 13), and the Companion's job is to make sure she discovers it in the right order. IV.10 Katherine's Elemental Anatomy · p.83 IV.12 Katherine's Concealment · p.150 FTE › IV.9 KATHERINE NICHOLAS IV.13 The Sister Reveal · p.153 IV.9 / 3 · 82 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY COMPARISON SPREAD · PART IV · FOR THEIR ENTERTAINMENT / REMNIA IV.10 KATHERINE'S ELEMENTAL ANATOMY Replace the Dyad label with the ruled anatomy. This is the most carefully ruled magic in FTE, and the development chapters get it wrong in instructive ways, so read this before touching any scene where Katherine's element changes. Katherine genuinely possesses Frost and Fire. CANON TCD-049. They aren't two ordinary channels. They're alternative expressions of one less-specialized ancestral Remnian elemental capacity, an older inheritance resurfacing in a modern body. From childhood that capacity organized itself around a dominant Frost expression, and Frost training reinforced it. CANON TCD-058. Ordinary stress and Fire exposure don't activate Fire. In FTE's climax, catastrophic severance or unavailability of her established Frost configuration lets the older capacity reroute through what modern Remnia calls Fire. No deliberate magical suppression is established. Zephyr is the contrast case. He is a genuine modern Fire/Frost Dyad. CANON TCD-049. The Sanctum would likely call Katherine a Dyad too, because its taxonomy has no better word; that diagnosis is incomplete or wrong (II.15). KATHERINE ZEPHYR Objective anatomy One ancestral capacity; alternative expressions Two modern channels, Fire and Frost Registered as Frost Flamer Institutional label "Dyad" (likely; wrong or incomplete) "Dyad" (correct) Before FTE Frost only, from childhood Two elements; 31–39 says since thirteen, after a training accident On the page Examination: Fire trauma, Frost response (ch. 7) Tundra: "orange fire edged with blue" in one hand, something else in the other; Melena sees both elements (ch. 18); Katherine sees them (ch. 17) What opens the second expression Catastrophic Frost failure, in the climax Already open FTE › IV.10 KATHERINE'S ELEMENTAL ANATOMY · CONTINUES → IV.10 / 1 · 83 SPINE 3SL ADST FTE TSWK CONTINUITY IV.10 · KATHERINE'S ELEMENTAL ANATOMY Fire caused by Melena? Status CONTINUED KATHERINE ZEPHYR No. Fire isn't borrowed, temporary, or a reaction to Melena (TCD-049) — CANON TCD-049, TCD-058 CANON TCD-049; drafting details SUPPORTED The Examination is the baseline. In ch. 7, Fire is on her body, the stress is extreme, her output spikes, and what comes out is Frost. The ruling was built on that scene. Any later scene in which ordinary fear, anger, heat, or grief pulls Fire out of her contradicts the drafted baseline, not just the ruling. AUTHOR RULING, 2026-09-26 TCD-058 clarified (no new TCD number assigned). CANON Heart's Mirror may foreshadow Katherine's hidden ancestral capacity through Fire imagery, fear, symbolic or ambiguous memory, lineage recognition, or other Mirror-generated material. Her Fire doesn't become objectively active during Challenge Five or its aftermath; she leaves the Mirror still operating through her Frost-dominant configuration. Material inside the Mirror isn't automatically an objective recovered memory. Her first objective Fire manifestation stays in the Challenge Six climax, after her established Frost pathway catastrophically fails or becomes unavailable and her broader capacity reroutes through the expression modern Remnia calls Fire. What the ruling does to FTE 31–39. The rewrite rule is simple: whatever the Mirror shows her, Katherine walks out with Frost and only Frost. DATE IV.10·W / 11 · 97 SPINE 3SL ADST FTE TSWK CONTINUITY LINEAGE · FIVE LAYERS · PART IV · FOR THEIR ENTERTAINMENT / REMNIA IV.11 LINEAGE: BIOLOGY, LAW, RECORD, SUCCESSION Keep four kinds of "family" in four columns. One fact about Katherine's family is settled, and it doesn't settle anything else. Tobias Lockwood is the shared biological father of Katherine Nicholas and Melena Lockwood. Katherine's biological parents are Michelle Nicholas and Tobias; Melena's are Lucia HastingsLockwood and Tobias. They are paternal half-sisters. CANON TCD-040. That truth doesn't independently establish Katherine's legal status, her records, her legitimacy, her title, her succession rights, or what the public knows. So the Companion keeps five layers apart, and every scene touching Katherine's parentage should know which layer it's operating in. Not ruled OPEN Recorded What do the documents say, and who holds them? The Sanctum is principal custodian of the records (TCD-059); their contents aren't ruled OPEN Public What do ordinary Remnians know? The Beta gives her "Lockwood amber eyes" and says Cordelia shares them. 3SL gives her green. FTE 31–39 has Tobias's eyes "the same shade as hers" and green. The Character Bible says emerald. The Remnian Timeline Before Story Start says green "associated with the Hastings lineage" and gives Elias golden Lockwood eyes; the unaudited Imperial Lineage repeats it. The Companion preserves the conflict (III.20). The Beta controls FTE's drafted page; nobody has ruled which book changes. REVISION FLAG (DEVELOPMENT CHAPTERS) The parents' elements. One Mirror line in 31–39 makes Tobias a Frost and Lucia a Flamer. The Beta has it the other way round: Lucia was "one of the most powerful" Frosts in the empire, Cordelia inherited her Frost ("Second generation"), and Melena "took after their father — fire running through her veins instead of ice" (ch. 8). The Beta controls; the Mirror lines need revising. Neither version says which side Katherine's ancestral capacity comes from (TCD-049). A parent's element isn't evidence of an inherited anatomy. Succession as the sources frame it. Welcome to Remnia describes a strictly matrilineal Hastings succession and a Crown that named Elias heir after Lucia's death to preserve its image. 31–39 has a Privy Council push Elias into the role. The Remnian Timeline Before Story Start (repeated in the unaudited Imperial Lineage) has Lucia decree at Melena's birth that daughters keep their place ahead of younger brothers. None of this is ruled. The one drafted fact is that Melena is second in line. GUARDRAIL Proving Tobias's paternity (IV.13) doesn't make Katherine a princess, a Lockwood, or an heir. Each of those is a separate decision, and some of them are the Crown's to make in-story. SPINE › PART II PART II · 26 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY SCHEMATIC · PART II · THE SHARED TANDEM SPINE II.2 MACRO GEOGRAPHY Place the lands relative to each other without inventing cartography. The four lands occupy multiple major landmasses and regions in one world. CANON TCD-025. Four placements are fixed: Remnia and Somnoria are separate continental landmasses with ocean between them. The Underside attaches to the Zenithia side, or corner, of Remnia's broader geography and descends from there. CANON TCD-025, TCD-026 Thalara, primarily underwater, lies farther outward in the island and sea region. Relative position is described by a Spain / France / Italy / Greek-islands comparison. That comparison controls relative placement only. GUARDRAIL What the analogy does not mean. It doesn't import real borders, coastlines, scale, distances, climate, culture, languages, or history. It doesn't make any land a disguised version of a real country. It doesn't settle which way anything faces on a map. It tells you roughly where the lands sit in relation to one another, and nothing more. The Underside's placement deserves its own sentence because it's the easiest to get wrong. It isn't a buried continent lying flat beneath Remnia. It's a vertically tiered nation that begins at or near the surface off Zenithia's side and descends. Its top tiers, Crownlight and Dawnveil, are above ground; by Ironcross the descent is fully subterranean. CANON TCD-026. Part III builds the full cross-section. SPINE › II.2 MACRO GEOGRAPHY · CONTINUES → II.2 / 1 · 27 3SL SPINE FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY II.2 · MACRO GEOGRAPHY CONTINUED Schematic, not a map. Until Danyelle's canonical Remnia map is reviewed, the Companion uses a relative-position schematic: two continental masses separated by ocean, the Underside marked as a descending column off one side of Remnia near Zenithia, and Thalara marked as a sea region farther out with its territory below the surface. The schematic carries no coastlines, scale bar, compass rose, or place names beyond these. SCHEMATIC · PLACEHOLDER Relative-position schematic pending review of Danyelle’s canonical Remnia map. No coastlines, scale, compass, or place names are drawn in this volume. OPEN DEVELOPMENT · MACRO CARTOGRAPHY · OPEN FIXED UNDECIDED One world; the four placements above; the Underside's vertical attachment off Zenithia's side (TCD-015, TCD-025, TCD-026). Coastlines, names of landmasses and oceans, scale, distances, islands, and borders. Zenithia's exact political classification is confirmed against the Remnian province index in Part IV rather than assumed here. WHY IT MATTERS DECIDE BY Distance sets travel time, and travel time sets how long news takes to cross (VII.2). Remnia layer when the Remnia map is reviewed; other layers safe to defer until a scene needs a distance. Why it matters: geography is where cross-book continuity breaks quietly. A character who reaches Somnoria "by nightfall" has just committed the Companion to a distance, whether anyone meant to. SOURCE SEE ALSO TCD-015, TCD-025, TCD-026. III.3 The Underside: A Vertical Nation → FTE concordance · p.216 IV.2 Remnia at a Glance · p.54 SPINE › II.2 MACRO GEOGRAPHY VI.2 Thalara at a Glance † II.2 / 2 · 28 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY EXPLAINER · LADDER · PART II · THE SHARED TANDEM SPINE II.4 KNOWLEDGE OF THE OTHER LANDS Kill universal-awareness shortcuts. There's no universal answer to "do people know about the other lands?" Awareness varies by nation, class, institution, profession, network, person, and time. CANON TCD-016. A smuggler, a Sanctum archivist, and a miner's kid in the same land can hold three different pictures, and all three can change between chapter 5 and chapter 25. The six levels from I.5 do the sorting: existence, location, functional, route, historical, and true Tandem knowledge. None implies another. Someone can know Somnoria's name and nothing about where it is. Someone can use a route without knowing what's on the far side of the boundary it crosses. Someone can know a land's history as their own institution teaches it, which may be the least reliable version available. Four kinds of source feed a character's picture, and the Companion tracks them separately: what their institution teaches or officially claims, what their network passes around as rumor or trade knowledge, what's been suppressed or rewritten, and what they've learned firsthand. An error counts as knowledge for drafting purposes. A character acts on what she believes, and the Companion records the belief with its lane label. What the drafted pages already establish. These are seed entries for the full matrix in VII.5. Every row is dated to its chapter; none is a claim about what the character's whole society knows. All four modern magical traditions ultimately draw from one primordial magical source or substrate that predates the modern division of the Tandem world. CANON TCD-035. The Divide didn't create four unrelated magics. Geography, environment, bodies, bloodlines, culture, survival, history, and institutions reshaped one shared power into systems that are now genuinely distinct, with their own access, costs, classifications, and cultural explanations. Both halves of that ruling carry weight. The shared root means cross-land resonance is possible without anyone inventing a coincidence. The genuine distinctness means a Somnorian dream technique doesn't work in Remnia just because both descend from the same source, and a Thalaran seal cadence isn't a Silencecraft seal with a different accent. Long isolation after the Divide accelerated the specialization. CANON TCD-045 THE FOUR MODERN BRANCHES LAND MODERN BRANCH Life, death, The Underside grief, and memory magic, formally organized as the Six Disciplines KNOWN PRACTICES AND TERMS WHO CLASSIFIES IT Thanomancy, Lamentcraft, Silencecraft, Underchant Cant, Funereal Engineering, Mycelial Memory ( SUPPORTED / PROVISIONAL, 3SL Full Worldbuilding). Onpage: Silencecraft and Silence seals, Quietcasters, Shrouds, funeral and grief thread, Hush, Underchant markings ( CURRENT DRAFTED TEXT, 3SL chs. 1–12). Graveshand is inherited Graves-family magic outside the Six Disciplines. "Severing" in the Silencecraft section of the worldbuilding and Graveshand Severance in 3SL are separate things until a source joins them. Each term is defined inside its own branch. SPINE › II.5 SHARED MAGICAL ANCESTRY · CONTINUES → II.5 / 2 · 33 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK II.5 · SHARED MAGICAL ANCESTRY CONTINUITY CONTINUED Resemblance isn't inheritance. The Underside treats memory as currency and names as anchors; Thalara's sea began holding memory after the Drowning, and shells hold impressions. The rhyme is real on the page. It isn't evidence of a shared mechanism, a shared origin event, or a transfer between lands. CANON Production Master A6 OPEN DEVELOPMENT · THE PRIMORDIAL LAYER · OPEN FIXED UNDECIDED One shared substrate; four genuinely distinct modern branches; isolation after the Divide accelerated specialization (TCD-035, TCD045). What the substrate was, how it was used before the Divide, the mechanism of divergence, which old principles survive in which branch, how branches interact when they meet, and which culture's theory of magic is most historically accurate. WHY IT MATTERS DECIDE BY Katherine's ancestral reversion (TCD-049) and every future cross-branch encounter lean on this layer. Safe to defer until a scene has to explain a cross-branch interaction rather than just show one. Why it matters: shared ancestry gives the series its deep logic, and false equivalence is the fastest way to ruin it. When two lands' magics meet on-page, the scene should feel like two dialects of a lost language, mutually strange, not two brands of the same tool. II.6 Crossblood: A Label, Not a Biology · p.35 III.8 Graveshand, Deathwork, and Severance † V.4 Dream Magic and the Dream Network † SPINE › II.5 SHARED MAGICAL ANCESTRY III.7 The Six Disciplines † IV.5 Elemental Magic · p.65 VI.3 The Seal Song and the Drowned Canticle † II.5 / 3 · 34 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY EXPLAINER · NOT THE SAME AS · PART II · THE SHARED TANDEM SPINE II.6 CROSSBLOOD: A LABEL, NOT A BIOLOGY Stop one institutional theory from becoming taxonomy. "Crossblood" is an institutional, scholarly, political, or historical label for people believed to carry inheritance associated with more than one separated Tandem tradition. It isn't an objectively correct universal biological category. CANON TCD-036. Institutions may define it differently through incomplete theory, bloodline politics, fear, or propaganda, and the label never establishes what a person's magic objectively is. The older two-world lore treated Crossbloods as a settled pre-fracture biology and offered a candidate list that grouped Katherine, Cassius, and Zephyr. That list is LEGACY / SUPERSEDED. Its three names don't share an anomaly: the rulings now explain Katherine and Zephyr in incompatible ways, and nothing established about Cassius puts him in either of their categories. NOT THE SAME AS Rare, ancient, hybrid, inherited, or anomalous magic is not Crossblood magic. "Crossblood" is a claim someone makes about a person. Whether it's true is a separate question, answered case by case. Case table. Every row stays unclassified until a case earns evidence. The "what's actually established" column shows why each person needs individual review instead of a shared label. PERSON WHAT'S ACTUALLY ESTABLISHED ABOUT THEIR MAGIC Katherine Nicholas Ancestral reversion to a less-specialized earlier Remnian elemental inheritance; Frost-dominant, with Fire as a rerouted alternative expression. CANON TCD049, TCD-058 Zephyr A genuine modern Fire/Frost Dyad. CANON TCD-049 OPEN · no case evidence Cassius Aether House Aether heir; his Labyrinth scar regulates bond synchronization. Which institutions use the term, how each defines it, what legal or social consequences follow, what each land calls the idea, and whether any real case ever fits. WHY IT MATTERS DECIDE BY A label with consequences is a plot engine. Someone called Crossblood by the Sanctum faces different stakes than someone called Crossblood by a Somnorian scholar. The first scene in which an institution applies the label to a named character. Why it matters: a catch-all label is seductive because it explains everything unusual at once. That's exactly what makes it dangerous in a series built on distinct magics. The Companion lets characters and institutions use the word. It doesn't let the narration believe them. A complete Prophecy of the Shattered Veil exists at the author and universe level. CANON TCD- 006. No intact, authoritative master copy of it is presently known in-world. CANON TCD-047. What the four lands have instead are four divergent historical descendants: Remnia's Breathbound Law, Somnoria's Dreamwalker's Writ, the Underside's Mourning Verse, and Thalara's Drowned Canticle. They aren't four physical pieces of one document. Nobody can assemble the prophecy by collecting them. What characters can do is reconstruct. Comparing the variants against historical, magical, material, and testimonial evidence can recover wording, underlying truth, or both. No national version is presumed untouched. CANON TCD-047 The author-level text. Reproduced exactly from The Poems. This is universe canon. It isn't in any character's hands. ```text The Prophecy of the Shattered Veil Before the stars were taught to burn, Before the tide was told to turn, A voice was born in blood and breath— A crown was forged, a vow to Death. The Order rose with chains of grace, To bind the world in rightful place. But power kept is power sown, And rot will bloom in gardens grown. They danced beneath a gilded stage, Their laughter hiding quiet rage. SPINE › II.13 THE SHATTERED VEIL AND ITS DESCENDANTS · CONTINUES → II.13 / 1 · 37 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK II.13 · THE SHATTERED VEIL AND ITS DESCENDANTS CONTINUITY CONTINUED They begged the flame to make things right, Then fed it truth and turned from light. They whispered wrongs they dared not speak, And carved their grief into the weak. They rose with crowns not earned but given— A curse beneath their blood was written. A dream unspooled across the sky, A mirror cracked, a perfect lie. A fate once fractured found its thread, But stitched its hope through knots of dread. These hollowed walls, now wrapped in mold, Remember what they can't behold. And from the ashes, sharp and bare— The truth still waits, but never fair. Four truths were cast to four-born lands, Each gripped by time's decaying hands. One held the storm with breath and bone— Their names were laws, their heirs were known. One broke the night to mine their dreams, And built their gods from shattered schemes. One walked where waves devoured the past, With songs too cursed to ever last. One slept beneath the smoking stone, And taught the dead to die alone. SPINE › II.13 THE SHATTERED VEIL AND ITS DESCENDANTS · CONTINUES → II.13 / 2 · 38 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK II.13 · THE SHATTERED VEIL AND ITS DESCENDANTS CONTINUITY CONTINUED Four branches bent by shadow's thread, Each blind to how the roots had bled. When time unravels spine from soul, When suns go dark and rivers toll, When every oath is stripped of name, And kin bear crowns they cannot claim— The veil will split. The mask will fall. No land will thrive. No voice will call. But in the ruin, cracked and cold, A single page remains to hold. Not war, nor flame, nor faith will win— But what was lost remembered in. And those who rise with open eyes Will curse the stars—and still survive. ``` (In the source file, "remembered" in the final stanza is italicized.) Four witnesses. The Elders' Prayer is one inherited magical working that survives in more than one culture under different language and ritual expression. CANON TCD-023. Its objective function is specific: it restores a living person's viable magical and life pattern when that pattern has been severed, corrupted, or catastrophically dysregulated. CANON TCD-057. Restored magical continuity can stabilize secondary bodily functions and call someone back from active dying, as long as a viable living pattern remains. It has hard edges. It can't resurrect completed death. It can't regenerate destroyed anatomy. CANON TCD-057 WHAT THE PRAYER CAN AND CAN'T DO CAN CAN'T Restore a viable living magical/life pattern that's been severed, corrupted, or catastrophically dysregulated Resurrect someone whose death is complete Stabilize secondary bodily functions when magical disruption is causal Regenerate destroyed anatomy. Isola's leg remains ruined despite life-saving stabilization. Call someone back from active dying while a viable pattern remains Redistribute a burden automatically and universally from one person to another Work through different cultural presentations with the same objective function Serve as a Frost-only ritual Two performances, about two years apart. Selah and Katherine perform the same inherited working independently, roughly two years apart. Across all four books, institutions and powerful people keep naming things they don't fully understand, and the names outlive the misunderstanding. The rulings have already caught enough of these that the pattern deserves its own page. Each row below is a case where a label circulating in-world diverges from objective truth. CLAIM IN CIRCULATION WHO HOLDS IT OBJECTIVE TRUTH RULING Katherine is a Dyad. The Sanctum (likely) One less-specialized ancestral Remnian capacity, Frost-dominant, with Fire as a rerouted alternative expression. Not Zephyr's kind of Dyad. TCD-049, TCD058 The House Aether piece is the original, whole Oneiros Prism. House Aether; some records One significant surviving piece of a fragmented artifact TCD-018 The Prism is Nightmare's cage. House Aether The entity exists beyond or behind a boundary the Prism helps maintain; it isn't inside the artifact TCD-038 The Prism is dream infrastructure. Somnorian working knowledge Accurate within scope, but a later application of a cross-land boundary artifact TCD-019 Crossblood is a biological category. Institutions and scholars A contested label, not proven biology TCD-036 The First Anchor was a willing adult. House Dren mythology The first currently established Anchor, Caelen, was twelve TCD-064 The fragment belongs to House Dren. Caelus A claim, not automatic objective truth TCD-039 The Drowning was one clean event that came without warning. Skyhold's official curriculum A staged regional catastrophe shaped by instability and political decisions TCD-055; Thalara Worldbuilding SPINE › II.15 RECURRING INSTITUTIONAL MISREADINGS · CONTINUES → II.15 / 1 · 45 3SL SPINE FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY II.15 · RECURRING INSTITUTIONAL MISREADINGS CONTINUED CLAIM IN CIRCULATION WHO HOLDS IT OBJECTIVE TRUTH RULING The Leviathan is a symbol. Skyhold's official curriculum A living pressure entity bound into seal infrastructure TCD-037, TCD055 Melena is Caelum's asset. Caelum A transactional alliance with coercive leverage on both sides TCD-043 The other lands are other worlds or realms. Isolated cultures One physical world; separate nations and territories TCD-015 Why the pattern matters for drafting: an institutional misreading is a knowledge state with a budget and an enforcement arm. The Sanctum doesn't just believe Katherine is a Dyad; it tests, files, and treats her as one. When the truth surfaces, the scene isn't only a revelation. It's an institution discovering that its own paperwork was wrong, and deciding what to do about the people who saw. GUARDRAIL Characters and institutions may use these labels freely in dialogue, documents, and POV narration filtered through them. The Companion's own narration never adopts them. OPEN DEVELOPMENT · WHY EACH INSTITUTION HOLDS ITS VERSION · OPEN FIXED UNDECIDED The objective truth in every row. Motive, where unruled: whether each misreading is honest error, convenient simplification, or deliberate suppression. WHY IT MATTERS DECIDE BY An honest error gets corrected when evidence arrives. A deliberate one gets defended. Before each institution's misreading is challenged on-page. The books are placed in time by macro windows, not by chapter lockstep. CANON TCD-013. 3SL happens about two years before the main concurrent period. ADST begins when Elias goes missing, and FTE begins a couple of weeks later. The two then run largely concurrently and end around the same time, without requiring their chapters to line up one for one. TSWK and the next Shadowmere installment form the next concurrent window. They begin directly after FTE and ADST, with no meaningful gap of months or years; a few days or small weeks for logistics are fine. They overlap each other substantially without needing identical opening dates. CANON TCD-014, TCD-024, TCD-054. The handoff is rolling: conditions for the later plots may already be active during late FTE and ADST, and still-later stories may begin activating before the prior pair fully resolves. Four bands per book. Every book is tracked by four separate dates, because "when does it happen?" has four answers. BOOK 3SL BACKGROUND ACTIVATION OPEN ADST Elias's years-long double life as Thorne; the organization's compromise of his research and dream functioning (TCD-032, TCD-062) FTE OPEN beyond the 3SL aftermath and the Elias clock NARRATIVE OPENING CLIMAX RESOLUTION About two years before the FTE/ADST window; exact date OPEN Remy's death and Melena's catastrophic Fire; Tiran's cleanup follows (TCD-002, TCD063) Silas and Isola on separate immediate paths (TCD044) When Elias goes missing (TCD013) The Nightmare organization's attempted Network rupture; the first established Convergence threshold, T0 (TCD-056, TCD-060) Around the same time as FTE's (TCD013) A couple of weeks after ADST opens (TCD-013) Challenge Six; the London Town forfeit (TCD-034, TCD-042, TCD-052) Around the same time as ADST's (TCD013) SPINE › II.16 SHARED CHRONOLOGY · CONTINUES → II.16 / 1 · 47 3SL SPINE FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY II.16 · SHARED CHRONOLOGY CONTINUED BOOK BACKGROUND ACTIVATION NARRATIVE OPENING TSWK May predate page one: the crackdown, Azrine's sanctuary, Levi's assignment (TCD-054) Directly after FTE/ADST; days or small weeks (TCD-054) OPEN (TCD-012) Next Shadowmere book May predate page one, including its character's continuing two-year life (TCD-054) Directly after FTE/ADST; overlaps TSWK substantially (TCD-054) OPEN CLIMAX RESOLUTION OPEN (TCD- 012) OPEN GUARDRAIL Temporal proximity isn't causation. Books that overlap in time don't cause each other's events unless a source says so. Local conflicts in TSWK and the next Shadowmere book stay independently rooted (TCD-054). OPEN DEVELOPMENT · THE CALENDAR · OPEN FIXED UNDECIDED The macro windows, the two-year interval, the FTE/ADST sequence, the direct rolling handoff, the Shadowmere 3 / 5 / 7 numbering pattern (TCD-013, TCD-014, TCD-054). A calendar anchor; exact dates in every band; the next Shadowmere installment's title and plot engine; the new canonical Remnian timeline (TCD-010). WHY IT MATTERS DECIDE BY Information travels at the speed of routes. Without dates, the Companion can't say whether a character could have heard news by a given chapter (VII.2). Remnian timeline before the FTE endgame fixes ages or years; the rest safe to defer until a cross-book appearance needs a date. A derived record, not a source. This record rebuilds the Remnian imperial and royal lineage from the sources, claim by claim. Each line names the source it comes from and carries the status that source earns under the authority order: rulings, then the Beta, then current author decisions, development, reference, shared lore, supporting artifacts, and legacy. It isn’t a source itself, and it settles nothing a ruling leaves open. Five layers stay apart, as IV.11 requires: biological, legal family status, public and recorded, title, and succession. A parent never fills a succession slot, and a title never proves a parent. Where two sources disagree, both stay on the page with their sources, and the disagreement gets a number (LC-nn) and a working block in lane D. LOCKED ANCHOR · CANON TCD-040 Tobias Lockwood is the biological father of Katherine (with Michelle Nicholas) and of Melena (with Lucia Hastings-Lockwood). Katherine and Melena are paternal half-sisters. That doesn’t make Katherine legally acknowledged, a Lockwood by law, a princess, publicly known, or an heir. TCD-059 governs the concealment only. GUARDRAIL Agreement between two weak sources isn’t canon. Two timelines that agree are still two timelines. The Beta has drafted twentyeight and a half chapters; the rulings have already decided who wins, how, why London Town forfeits, what Katherine's magic is, how the sisters find each other, and where Atlas's injury comes from. What the rulings haven't done is write the scenes. This dossier holds both halves: the drafted book as it stands, and the ruled endgame it still has to reach. REMNIA IV.1 BOOK IDENTITY AND SOURCE STATUS Separate canonized prose from usable development. The Beta is the book. CANON TCD-001. Everything outside it, including the two newest development files, has to be rewritten into the Beta's generation before it becomes canon. That rule does real work in FTE, because the development chapters are good, specific, and in places flatly contradict rulings made after they were written. STATUS · AUTHOR CORRECTION 2026-10-09 TCD-025 · TCD-041 CANON An empire on its own continental landmass, separated from Somnoria by ocean; the Crown at the highest public civil/dynastic level of the sovereignty structure. DRAFTED Seventeen provinces. FTE Beta Remnia is an empire on its own continental landmass, separated from Somnoria by ocean; the Crown sits at the highest public civil/dynastic level of the Remnian sovereignty structure. CANON TCD-025, TCD-041. The Beta gives it seventeen provinces and confirms the count in three places: Melena's cover story is a tour of "all seventeen" provinces (ch. 2), the Delegation hall has seventeen archways "one for each province of the Empire", and seventeen Sanctum officials attend the Maze. ( CURRENT DRAFTED TEXT) Its years are counted ER. The Empire's borders are closed. Caelum says so in ch. 2 ("The Remnian Empire may have closed its borders, but there are still ways through"), and Melena, until he tells her otherwise, believes "There's nothing outside of Remnia anymore." That line is a character belief, dated to ch. 2, not a statement of what Remnia teaches (II.4). Places on the page. Only what the sources actually put somewhere. She has lived in the Underside for two years, yet in ch. 2 she answers Caelum's mention of Somnoria with "That's not a Remnian province… There's nothing outside of Remnia anymore." Both can be true only if she thinks of Shadowmere as something other than "outside": Remnianadjacent, a lawless borderland, or not a nation at all. The page doesn't say which. OPEN · decide before any Melena POV scene names what the Underside is. SPELLING FLAG The Beta writes "Alleia" three times; TCD-027 writes "Alea." This is a fine-detail spelling choice the audit leaves open, not a geography question. Pick one before the Remnia map is reviewed. Welcome to Remnia says seventeen provinces in its text, lists eighteen names in its table, and says the Elemental Court controls "8 of 17" while marking nine. The Beta's seventeen controls. Precise provincial positions wait for Danyelle's Remnia map (TCD-027); nothing here places a province on a compass. SOURCE SEE ALSO II.2 Macro Geography · p.27 II.4 Knowledge of the Other Lands · p.29 III.3 The Underside: A Vertical Nation → FTE concordance · p.216 NOTES · IV.2 FTE › IV.2 REMNIA AT A GLANCE IV.2 / 3 · 56 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY POWER MAP · PART IV · FOR THEIR ENTERTAINMENT / REMNIA IV.3 POWER AND JURISDICTION Replace "who's in charge" with a real power map. Remnia doesn't have one ruler. It has several centers of authority that overlap, compete, and sometimes need each other. CANON TCD-041. The Crown is the highest public civil and dynastic authority and directly controls London Town and imperial affairs. Provincial Leaders administer their territories under titles that vary. The Provincial Leadership Board gives the provinces formal representation, and a Conqueror's one-year seat can change it. The Elemental Court is a separate political-magical bloc with influence over several provinces and over elemental policy, but not over London Town. The Sanctum is semi-autonomous in magical regulation, bloodlines, Tournament law, doctrine, security, and historical control. It isn't simply the Crown's agency, and it isn't legally omnipotent over the Crown either. The practical consequence for drafting: when something goes wrong in FTE, the first question is never "who's in charge?" It's "whose jurisdiction is this, and who else thinks it's theirs?"
