The Sanctum is the institution FTE is actually about. It regulates magic, bloodlines, Tournament law, doctrine, security, and historical control. CANON TCD-041. It runs the Tournament in public as a sacred tradition and a political reward, and in private uses it to classify, cultivate, control, exploit, monitor, suppress, or eliminate dangerous people. CANON TCD-034. It is the principal enforcer of Katherine's concealment and custodian of the records. CANON TCD-059. It also doesn't get to be the villain of everything. The rulings are careful: the Sanctum accepts and may exploit the London Town forfeit but doesn't cause it (TCD-042, TCD-052), and it didn't originate Katherine's erasure (TCD-059). An institution that frightening is more frightening when it's also bounded. What the Beta shows the Sanctum doing. ( CURRENT DRAFTED TEXT) FUNCTION ON THE PAGE Membership Members are "chosen from the highest ranks of each province, then stripped of their identities"; officials wear black and gold, and gray robes at the Maze Selection Places Pieces at Delegation "by the power vested in the Sanctum"; runs full identity scans of candidates Ownership of Pieces Nico: "The moment you stepped off the Board at Delegation, you stopped belonging to Old Prosperity. You belong to the Tournament now. To the Sanctum." Surveillance The Houses' common areas are monitored around the clock, audio and visual; the Sanctum reviews all footage and broadcasts select clips Challenge design Builds Challenges from Piece performance data; fields non-human combatants and constructs in Challenge One Discipline Sanctum Protocol 17 (Melena and Cordelia's violation, chs. 8–9); Code Sixteen; probation for Jarom Fairchild and Olivia Rembrandt after the augmenter incident (ch. 12) Presence Seventeen officials at the Maze; sixteen hand out blindfolds; the seventeenth is taller, with robes "shimmering faintly wrong" (ch. 22) Handlers Nico for Old Prosperity; Raven for London Town, who carries neutralizing cuffs and a suppressor rod FTE › IV.4 THE SANCTUM · CONTINUES → IV.4 / 1 · 62 SPINE 3SL ADST FTE TSWK IV.4 · THE SANCTUM CONTINUITY CONTINUED Code Sixteen, as the Beta quotes it: "No Piece shall wield, develop, or demonstrate a second elemental affinity beyond the one registered at birth, whether innate, enhanced, or artificially induced. Penalty: immediate removal from the Tournament and Imperial discipline." "Imperial law didn't bother splitting hairs. If a second element answered when you called, you were in violation." What the development chapters add. ( SUPPORTED / PROVISIONAL · FTE 31–39) The Sanctum writes the Naming Ceremony scripts, which King Pieces "approve" and Queen Pieces read (ch. 32). Elite mages administer Heart's Mirror. Pieces are isolated before Challenge Six. Ceiling cameras show a tally light that never truly goes off. A no-kill order is issued for Challenge Six, then a non-verbal yield is ruled invalid and a surrendering Piece dies (ch. 39). REVISION FLAG (DEVELOPMENT CHAPTERS) The penalty for a second element. FTE 31–39 ch. 38 says dualists have been illegal "since the Faction Wars" and that "the punishment for being a dualist was death." The Beta's Code Sixteen says removal from the Tournament and Imperial discipline. The Beta controls. "Imperial discipline" can be as dark as the book needs, but it has to be introduced as that, not as a death statute. THE SANCTUM IN EACH KNOWLEDGE LANE. Remnian magic is elemental, organized into factions, and registered at birth. It descends from the same primordial substrate as the other three lands' magic and is now its own system, with its own access, costs, and classifications. CANON TCD-035. Nothing in this section makes a Remnian element equivalent to a Somnorian or Underside practice (II.5). The seven factions. The roster and descriptions come from FTE Elemental Factions ( SUPPORTED). The right-hand column is what the Beta actually shows. II.5 Shared Magical Ancestry · p.32 II.6 Crossblood: A Label, Not a Biology · p.35 IV.10 Katherine's Elemental Anatomy · p.83 FTE › IV.5 ELEMENTAL MAGIC IV.5 / 4 · 68 SPINE 3SL ADST FTE TSWK CONTINUITY SYSTEM EXPLAINER · PART IV · FOR THEIR ENTERTAINMENT / REMNIA THE TOURNAMENT: STRUCTURE AND PURPOSE IV.6 Explain both faces of the Tournament. The Chess Tournament pits two provinces against each other with living Pieces on a chessboard's positions. This year it's Old Prosperity against London Town. The Empress's opening letter sets the public terms: thirty-two Pieces, "sixteen from each province, eight men and eight women"; the goal is to "capture the opposing King. Achieve Checkmate. Win."; and "to the victor, I offer a seat on the Provincial Leadership Board — a year of power, a year of favor, a year to reshape your province's future." ( CURRENT DRAFTED TEXT, front matter) That's the public half. The private half is canon too: the Sanctum uses selection, assignments, challenges, stress, injuries, alliances, and survival to classify, cultivate, control, exploit, monitor, suppress, or eliminate dangerous people. CANON TCD-034. Neither half cancels the other. The Board seat is real, and so is the cull. The Tournament is designed around seven Challenges, and reaching the Seventh isn't guaranteed. Attrition, Checkmate, disqualification, intervention, or another decisive outcome can end it early. In the current FTE, the Sanctum declares Challenge Six decisive and terminates the Tournament. CANON TCD-007 REVISION FLAG (FTE BETA) "Six challenges." The Empress's letter calls the Tournament "survival distilled into six challenges." TCD-007 makes the design seven, with the Sixth ending this one. The letter's wording needs revising to fit the ruling. It doesn't compete with it. HOW IT RUNS, AS DRAFTED. STAGE ON THE PAGE Candidacy Twenty is within the "Tournament candidate range"; High Command's order to Katherine arrives under General Gareth Thorne's signature Delegation Candidates are assessed, then placed on the Board at a ceremony "by the power vested in the Sanctum"; Delegation members are exempt from the identity scan FTE › IV.6 THE TOURNAMENT: STRUCTURE AND PURPOSE · CONTINUES → IV.6 / 1 · 69 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY IV.6 · THE TOURNAMENT: STRUCTURE AND PURPOSE CONTINUED STAGE ON THE PAGE Houses Each province lives in a Havenwood House under a handler; common areas are filmed around the clock Welcome Feast Seventeen tables; the first time Katherine and Melena share a room Challenges Designed by the Sanctum; briefed by handlers; broadcast No-kill order "You are not to intentionally kill another Piece" (Nico, before the Maze; Katherine invokes a "no killing rule" in Challenge One). II.13 The Shattered Veil and Its Descendants · p.37 IV.16 Atlas: The Synchronization Injury · p.163 IV.17 The Elders' Prayer in FTE · p.166 IV.20 The Endgame: Forfeit and Command Authority · p.176 FTE › IV.7 THE CHALLENGES IV.7 / 5 · 76 SPINE 3SL FTE ADST TSWK CONTINUITY RULES TABLE · PART IV · FOR THEIR ENTERTAINMENT / REMNIA IV.8 CONQUEROR RULES Define the title correctly. The Conqueror isn't the King, and isn't whoever lands the last hit. The Conqueror is the surviving Piece whose decisive sanctioned action earns victory, and that Piece holds the winning province's Provincial Leadership Board seat for the next year. CANON TCD-034. Under a forfeit, the Conqueror is the surviving Piece whose final sanctioned action or matchup directly produces the decisive state that causes the forfeit. Katherine Nicholas is this Tournament's Conqueror. CANON TCD-034. Atlas and Tiran's confrontation is officially a draw by intervention; Katherine and Melena produce the decisive sequence. SITUATION WHO IS CONQUEROR Ordinary Checkmate The surviving Piece whose decisive sanctioned action earns it; not automatically the King STATUS CANON TCD-034; the precise Checkmate definition OPEN CANON TCD-034 Forfeit The surviving Piece whose final sanctioned action or matchup directly produces the decisive state This Tournament Katherine, because her sanctioned action against or for Melena prevents catastrophe, saves or stabilizes Melena, and creates the decisive state The last physical attack before an intervention Doesn't decide it; Atlas doesn't win by landing it CANON TCD-034 The Conqueror is dead or incapacitated Not ruled OPEN CANON TCD-034, TCD- 042 The Beta's own words fit the ruling: Old Prosperity's mess hall hangs banners "embroidered with the names of the Conquerors who had led Old Prosperity to glory," and Part Three of the Beta is titled THE CONQUEROR. Michelle Nicholas "secured one of Old Prosperity's greatest Tournament victories"; whether she was its Conqueror isn't stated. FTE › IV.8 CONQUEROR RULES · CONTINUES → IV.8 / 1 · 77 SPINE 3SL ADST FTE TSWK CONTINUITY IV.8 · CONQUEROR RULES CONTINUED GUARDRAIL The Empress's letter says the goal is to capture the King. That's the public rule, and the Beta should keep saying it. It isn't how this Tournament ends, and the Conqueror rule already covers why. POSSIBLE PRECEDENT POSSIBLE The Factions file describes a 2092 ER Tournament whose last two survivors forfeited rather than accept victory, and an Empire that covered it up. If recovered, it would give the London Town forfeit an institutional memory. It isn't canon, and its survivor Zephyr Windrider has nothing to do with Zephyr Augustine. OPEN DEVELOPMENT · CONQUEROR BYLAWS · OPEN FIXED UNDECIDED Definition; forfeit rule; Katherine as Conqueror; the Atlas–Tiran draw (TCD-034). Checkmate and "final move" definitions; deliberate setups; death, incapacity, proxy, and succession; the title's effect on rank; the Board seat's actual powers; what the winning province gains. WHY IT MATTERS DECIDE BY A Corporal on the Leadership Board is a political earthquake. What the seat can do decides what Katherine's victory costs everyone else. Katherine's exact sanctioned action before the climax; Board powers before the aftermath.
